                                                          38. Stopping Procedure

                                                             After the record has been completed, operate
                                                          the recorder with the SIGNAL SELECTOR
                                                          switch in the S. C. position to obtain a zero check.
                                                          Stop the recorder by throwing the power switch
                                                          to the POWER OFF position. Advance the
                                                          chart, with the manual advance knob, until the
                                                          zero trace is approximately halfway past the lower
                                                          edge of the tear-off scale; leave enough of the zero
                                                          trace to indicate correct calibration. Carefully
                                                          rip the chart against the lower edge of the tear-
                                                          off scale.

CHAPTER 5

FIELD MAINTENANCE

       Note. This chapter contains information for field maintenance. The amount     of repair  that can be performed by
units having field maintenance responsibility is limited only by the tools and test  equipment  available, and by the skill
of the repairman.

ee

                   Section I. TROUBLESHOOTING AT FIELD MAINTENANCE LEVEL

      Warning: When servicing the equipment, be extremely careful; high voltage is present. Keep one
hand in your pocket when measuring socket voltages. After voltage is turned off, short-circuit the part
to circuit ground before touching any part of the equipment with your hands.

             CONTROL PANEL

             CONVERTER                                                                                                       80. Troubleshooting Chart
              AMPLIFIER
                                                                                                                                The following chart is supplied as an aid in
 SIGNAL AND  FREQUENCY-TIME                                                                                                  locating trouble in the radiosonde recorder. The
LINE INPUTS         RECORDER                                                                                                 chart lists the symptoms that the repairman ob-
                                                                                                                             serves, either aurally or visually, while making a
                                                                                                                             few simple tests. Once the trouble has been
                                                                                                                             localized to a stage or circuit, a tube check and
                                                                                                                             voltage and resistance measurements of this stage
                                                                                                                             or circuit should be sufficient to isolate the de-
                                                                                                                             fective part. Normal voltage and _ resistance
                                                                                                                             measurements are shown in figures 72 and 73.
                                                                                                                             Wave forms obtained at various test points
                                                                                                                             throughout the equipment are shown in figure 71.
                                                                                                                             Location of parts of major components of the
                                                                                                                             recorder are shown in figures 50 through 67 and
                                                                                                                             the wiring diagrams of the converter amplifier,
                                                                                                                             power supply, and control panel are shown in fig-
                                                                                                                             ures 74 through 76. Figures 77 through 82 are
                                                                                                                             the schematic diagrams of the converter ampli-
                                                                                                                             fier, control panel, frequency-time recorder (both

                                                                                                                             models), power supply, and fan panel. Figures
                                                                                                                             83 and 84 are the schematic diagrams of the cabi-

                                                                                                                             nets. The resistor and capacitor color codes are
                                                                                                                             shown in figures 69 and 70 respectively.

   a. Smash. Smash the controls, tubes, coils,          d. Bend. Bend panels, cabinets, and chassis.
                                                        e. Hxplode. If explosives are necessary, use
switches, capacitors, transformers, loudspeaker,     firearms, grenades, or TNT.
and printing mechanism; use sledges, axes, hand-        f. Dispose. Bury or scatter the destroyed parts
axes, pickaxes, hammers, crowbars, or heavy tools.   in slit trenches, fox holes, or other holes, or throw

                                                     them into streams.
                                                        g. Destroy. Destroy everything.
